# DNF90 Operation Log

## 2026-08-17 - clear false reconnect state before first-character selection

- A fresh empty selector can arm the current `op2/31` channel-reconnect probe before the real three-byte `GET_USERINFO op8` reaches the shared roster bootstrap. The existing roster fix marked `rosterRequested` but retained an already-armed `channelReconnect`, so the following first-character `op4` could still enter the reconnect route and reproduce the obsolete endpoint/scene lifecycle around `op24`.
- The authoritative roster bootstrap now clears only an unbound reconnect guess: no character may be selected and neither a town nor dungeon scene may exist. An already-bound channel reconnect remains unchanged. Regressions cover both the empty-selector correction and the bound-reconnect guard, then require the ordinary selection stream to contain no reconnect resident notice.
- Replaced the release-oriented root README with the concise developer startup flow: install the five client files, start/configure/status/login, register, and enter with the first character. The old second-character workaround is no longer documented as expected behavior.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, every `internal/modules/dnf/...` test, the focused first-character/reconnect tests, the complete `dnfbridge` suite excluding the macOS-unavailable `127.0.0.2` bind test, the control suite excluding its two Windows-path-semantics tests, and the required `go vet` set. A Windows/amd64 candidate built successfully at `46,913,536` bytes with SHA256 `732C6B25ABD2473BB8B08BCB0B4FAEB822F13D0BA23AD92BCB81D71DB8BAF020`; it was not installed. No runtime executable, database, PVF, generated configuration, or client DLL changed. Fresh Windows-client acceptance is still required.

## 2026-08-06 - merge damage-font protocol and dungeon entry-level repair

- Selectively merged the supplied damage-font source without replacing newer inventory, party, scene, or repository work. The current client `op515` now validates the exact 19-byte action-162 request against the runtime PVF `[add damage font skin]` definition, consumes one source stack and persists character-scoped ownership in one character-asset transaction. It supports PVF `[period]`, `[date]`, and `[unlimit]` expiration contracts; failed validation or persistence leaves the source stack unchanged.
- Added `op1288` selection against durable, unexpired ownership and class-0 `NOTI 1239` projection (`selected`, count, then font-index/expiry entries). The visible sequence after a successful unlock is class-1 `op515` ACK, repository-backed source `op14` refresh, then NOTI 1239. The selected scene tail also projects the current damage-font state on login/reconnect after the actor-owned stores are ready.
- Merged the `op16` level gate without regressing the current party preflight or layered-dungeon runtime: a `dnfdungeon.ErrEntryLevelTooLow` result now sends only the proved class-1 failure body `{0,14,0}` and creates no runtime or scene-bootstrap state.
- Added protocol, atomic rollback, expiry, PVF, live `Script.pvf`, NOTI-layout, and insufficient-level entry regressions. `gofmt`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, all `internal/modules/dnf/...` tests, the complete `dnfbridge` suite, controller tests, required `go vet`, and the real-PVF damage-font audit passed. Built but did not install `runtime/tmp/DNF90Server-damage-font-6f581375-9a63-4370-a4e7-48e0fa43a334.exe` (46,904,832 bytes). The `control.bat check --skip-database` preflight deliberately refused while the existing DNF90 PID `23188` is running; no runtime configuration, live executable, service process, database state, or PVF asset was changed.

## 2026-08-05 - merge linked-objective completion and read-only clear-quest fixes

- Merged the linked-task repair from the supplied `op33/op34` archive without regressing current quest replay, expert-job, or bulk `op1429` behavior. A no-reward linked child callback now atomically completes the child and recomputes its parent, returning the current-client sequences `op34 -> op574 -> op356` for a child callback and `op33 -> op574 -> op356` for a parent callback. These linked child completions grant neither character experience nor invented reward items.
- Merged the `op356` repair as projection-only behavior. Clear-quest snapshots, persisted-town login transitions, and town transport no longer create or complete PVF prerequisite quests. A town destination with an unmet prerequisite is blocked with no response packet, location mutation, or quest write; routes without prerequisites remain available.
- Added regression coverage for linked-objective protocol order and atomic state, clear-quest read-only projection, unmet teleport/map-station/cross-town prerequisites, and persisted-town login bitmap output. `gofmt`, package listing, all `internal/modules/dnf/...` tests, complete `dnfbridge` tests, controller tests, and required `go vet` passed. A candidate server binary was built under `runtime/tmp`; deployment preflight was intentionally blocked by its running-service safety guard, so no runtime configuration, live binary, database state, or service process changed.

## 2026-08-05 - make random-reward firecrackers use the ordinary consumable path

- The Spring Celebration Firecracker and Knight Joust Firecracker were sent through the current client's ordinary right-click `op44` path, but the inventory owner admitted only `[waste]` templates. Their PVF type is `[random reward item]`, so the bridge correctly avoided a fabricated ACK but the player saw no decrement or result.
- `op44` now resolves this one consumable family from the active `Script.pvf` `[chn random image percent]` table. The two Spring definitions (`490007317` and `490701730`) consume one firecracker for a 99,000/100,000 visual-only outcome or a 1,000/100,000 grant of `490701733`; Knight Joust Firecracker `490005593` uses its proved 80,000/100,000 visual-only outcome or 20,000/100,000 grant of `490005595`.
- The response remains the current-client native sequence `op44 ACK -> class-0/op14 source row -> class-0/op14 reward row when present`; no random-box result-window protocol is introduced. A reward is placed with its PVF item metadata in the valid main-inventory range. If a positive outcome cannot be placed, the entire transaction rolls back, preserving the source firecracker rather than losing an earned result.
- Added unit coverage for PVF table parsing, source decrement plus reward placement, and full-inventory rollback. The real-PVF smoke test verifies all three live source definitions and their exact weights/reward IDs. Package listing, all DNF-module tests, the complete `dnfbridge` suite, controller tests, required `go vet`, and deployment preflight pass.
- Built and installed `runtime/bin/DNF90Server.exe` (SHA256 `CF11E8789A2F6A6A79D12E1B0EE3B8DC3EE5386DE2B41D79089DEB453EE0BFB6`) solely through `deploy/windows/control.bat`. A stale project `DNF90Launcher.exe` process initially held its identical old backup; after it was closed, the controller finalized the verified atomic install. DNF90Server is READY as PID `10092`, portable MySQL is READY as PID `34772`, and all 120 listener probes pass. No PVF, database item state, or handwritten generated runtime configuration was modified.

## 2026-08-05 - make mailbox select-all fill available slots without crashing the current client

- The live op95 reproduction claimed mail `58`, then sent a clear-first class0/op97 snapshot followed by five full class0/op13 container pages while the mailbox UI was still active. The client faulted in `DNF.exe` at `0x027082D3` (`0xC0000005`, read of `0x41`) before it consumed the following private op413 payload; DNF90Server remained live and only observed the remote socket close. Mail claims now retain the required op95 ACK and op97 mailbox reload, but project only the durable changed main-inventory rows through current class0/op14, so the unsupported post-claim full-container replay and its combat-power side effect are removed.
- Batch op95 extraction is now per-mail atomic instead of all-or-nothing for the entire select-all request. It fills compatible stacks first, then empty quick slots `3..8`, then the PVF-resolved item page. Every mail that fully fits is committed with its gold and attachments; a later full mail remains unclaimed with all of its assets intact. If none fit, the existing inventory-full error remains. This preserves atomicity for each individual letter and lets a single select-all request use every available quick-slot and backpack position.
- Added regression coverage for the live-safe incremental refresh boundary, quick-slot-first placement, partial select-all success through both quick slots and the item tab, retention of the first unplaceable mail (including gold), and the all-full rejection path. `gofmt`, package listing, all DNF-module tests, the complete bridge suite, controller tests, and required `go vet` pass. Built but did not install `runtime/tmp/DNF90Server-mail-select-all-candidate.exe` (SHA256 `3414B98F134A33F7411EF33033905586801027EA4EB41390E1E6EC981E3663CC`). `control.bat check --skip-database` correctly refused its mutating preflight while DNF90 was live; `control.bat status` confirms the existing service remains READY with all 120 listeners. No database, PVF, client asset, generated runtime configuration, or live executable was changed.

Binding and an existing item-instance deadline remain on the transformed bead; the new template metadata, item ID, stack count, and any PVF result deadline come from the selected result row.

Crystal-contract cubes retain their existing op338 path and dungeon ownership rules.
- The active ticket definitions `490007246` and `490701734` are resolved without hardcoded bead IDs. A conditional real-PVF smoke test asserts their 91-member A/result pools and `3037 x10` material requirement when `DNFBRIDGE_REAL_PVF_SMOKE` is supplied; focused transaction and bridge tests cover successful mutation, split material consumption, invalid/missing inputs, village-only enforcement, and crystal-contract fallback.

## 2026-08-05 - rewrite online parties around one authoritative manager

- Reworked the DNF90 party lifecycle against the current 86JP reference commit `069ad4f1fb9a96ce4fd824d3412b5951f07bfe2f`, while keeping the existing Go composition boundaries and current-EXE packet layouts. The C# transport and packets were not copied: `internal/modules/dnf/party` now owns the central, process-local membership graph, and `dnfbridge` remains responsible for compatibility packets, session validation, scene/roster projection, directory, raid integration, and the directed UDP relay.
- Party IDs are allocated independently of character IDs; membership has four stable slots; a character is atomically detached from an old party before it joins another; and invitations bind both participants' live session generations. Leader leave or leader transfer retires the old party identity, clears the old client table, and rebuilds survivors under a fresh party ID with the successor in slot zero. Reconnect replaces only the session generation, retaining the party/slot and rejecting packets from the retired socket.
- Party directory, peer invitation/accept, leave/kick/reorder/leader transfer, raid-derived parties, and relay topology now all resolve through this one manager. Per-session party state is reduced to a current-EXE packet cache; the previous divergent pending-invite and session-owned membership helpers have been removed. The existing 86JP-style directed UDP relay remains private-LAN-only in this local profile and advertises the configured local game address rather than a public or wildcard endpoint.
- Added regressions for leader rebuild, stable slots, generation-bound invitations and reconnect, cross-party joining with old-party survivor refresh, four-member capacity, reordering, and idempotent first-lobby creation. `gofmt`, package listing, every DNF-module test, the complete `dnfbridge` and controller suites, required `go vet`, production server build, offline BAT preflight, and live readiness all passed.
- Per the standing force-update instruction, the prior server was preserved at `runtime/backups/server/20260805-1035-party-manager-rewrite-predeploy/DNF90Server.exe` with SHA256 `AB6068D8F7F0DA2BC7D3DA0FDD7CC7B61362729AEBB2E4E09DF6410F0C26604C`. The installed `runtime/bin/DNF90Server.exe` SHA256 is `96A0F0E8611B381D93BE588986149957595F08C6CFBB69F5EDA2910D8EA39A91`; portable MySQL and DNF90Server are READY and all 120 game listeners accept connections. ## 2026-08-05 - harden the current-client party lifecycle against reconnect races

- Compared the local party implementation with 86JP main commit `77fd463ca6426489d22ef625642d155e4db6ed26` (2026-08-04, `complete party networking and social lookup`). The existing DNF90 bridge already has the relevant current-EXE wire layouts for four-member formation, party directory, invite/accept, leave/kick, leader rebuild, scene fan-out, dungeon follow, and dynamic `class0/op11` peer-port publication.
- Adopted the missing lifecycle protection without copying C# packets: every pending invite is now bound to both authoritative `gameSession` instances and their selected-character generations. A response after the inviter reconnects cannot attach the acceptor to the replacement session. All state-changing online-party commands are also rejected when they arrive from a retired connection, so stale buffered packets cannot join, leave, kick, delegate, reorder, or change a current party.
- A generation is now allocated before a session is entered into the online-character directory, including focused/reconnect paths that precede the event loop. The exact 22-byte `op11` endpoint layout remains unchanged, but remote member rows now use a server-owned directed UDP relay: every ordered pair gets its own port, learns only the first datagram whose IPv4 matches that member's authenticated TCP peer, and forwards through the reverse pair socket. A partial or stale room is never advertised; the existing direct endpoint form remains only as the safe fallback when a complete relay room cannot be installed.
- The relay binds only the concrete configured game-channel IPv4 (currently the auto-detected private LAN address), never a wildcard. `server.partyUdpRelayPortStart` and `server.partyUdpRelayPortCount` reserve a default range of `30000-30063`. A future public profile must advertise its public relay IPv4 and expose that same UDP range (with separate bind/advertise addresses when it is behind NAT); this local-only profile intentionally continues to advertise only a private LAN address. Added regressions for inviter replacement, stale direct-join suppression, directed relay forwarding/source authentication, and former-member leg removal.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, package listing, every DNF-module test, the complete `dnfbridge` and controller suites, required `go vet`, and the production build. Per the force-update instruction, DNF, launcher, and old server were stopped; the prior server/control binaries were preserved under `runtime/backups/server/20260805-093635-party-relay-predeploy`. `runtime/bin/DNF90Server.exe` is now SHA256 `AB6068D8F7F0DA2BC7D3DA0FDD7CC7B61362729AEBB2E4E09DF6410F0C26604C`; its startup confirms `party_udp_relay=true`, host `192.168.5.7`, range `30000-30063`, and all 120 current TCP listener probes pass. No client DLL, PVF, generated configuration, or database row was hand-edited.

## 2026-08-04 - complete the all-day knight joust tournament, settlement, and history flow

- Completed the current-EXE class-0 activity protocol from the native consumers instead of retaining the opening-only placeholder. Op1243 now uses the exact 31-byte `{u16 round,u8 stage,7*DWORD match}` projection proved by `sub_E72600`; each match DWORD is `{winner_id,winner_action,loser_id,loser_action}` as consumed by `sub_F34350`, ordered as four quarter-finals, two semi-finals, and one final. Op1293 now returns up to 500 exact seven-byte `{u16 round,u8 winner,f32 multiplier}` rows as proved by `sub_F29A80`, followed by the client's zero-sequence sentinel padding.
- Added a restart-stable seven-match tournament simulation over the active `event/chn_event/chn_joust.evt`. The eight-rider draw and unbiased winner decisions are deterministic per round because the PVF exposes no hidden strength/win-probability field; each winner/loser action is selected from the rider's real four-by-seven Win/Loss cumulative tables using the opponent's proved attack category `0/1/27/28`. Malformed attack categories or cumulative tables fail closed. Op1241 history counters are reconstructed from the preceding 500 deterministic tournaments.
- Implemented the original two-hour cadence as an all-day repeating activity: betting `0..90` minutes, quarter-final stage at 90, semi-final at 95, final stages at 100/105, settlement at 110, and the next opening at 120. The process gameplay queue schedules only those boundaries. Op1240 is emitted once at real state transitions `1/2/3/4`; 95- and 105-minute animation steps emit only op1243, preventing the previously observed op1291/start-notice feedback flood.
- The public pool now aggregates durable ledgers from all 32 character slots on the authenticated local account, including already-settled ledgers for the still-active round, so one character's settlement cannot change another character's multiplier. Betting remains limited to one rider and 10,000 crystals per character/round, closes after 90 minutes, awards one PVF participation firework per wagered crystal, refreshes the authoritative inventory, and republishes the changed op1241/op1242 pool to every online session for that account without replaying op1240.
- Added atomic, idempotent settlement through `CharacterAssets`. Winning claims grant `floor(wager * final_multiplier)` permanent竞猜硬币 item `490005585`; losing claims only close the ledger. The pending flag and inventory change commit together, so inventory-full failures preserve the claim for reconnect retry and a committed claim cannot pay twice. A previous pending round must settle before a new bet can overwrite it. Completed-round winner/multiplier overrides are persisted in account settings before settlement and merged into the deterministic 500-row history after restart.
- Reconnect/town bootstrap now sends the current state, roster/pool, and the appropriate progressive op1243 snapshot; it also retries any due account settlement and refreshes the winning character's main inventory before the event snapshot. The background scheduler settles all account characters when an online session crosses the 110-minute boundary and pushes the authoritative inventory before state 4.
- Synthetic protocol/domain regressions cover exact op1243 byte order, all phase boundaries, account-wide pool aggregation independent of `pending`, three-firework placement for a three-crystal wager, deterministic history float rows, winner payout, settlement rollback, history persistence, and duplicate-settlement prevention. ## 2026-08-03 - add reload-safe Lua event listener registration

- Added the pure-Lua `dnf90.on(event_name, callback)`, `dnf90.once(event_name, callback)`, and `dnf90.off(listener_id)` interfaces on top of the existing `90CNLua/1.3.0` native lifecycle queue. The first revision accepts only the proved `ui_ready` and `ui_closed` names, assigns process-local positive listener IDs, and preserves the existing direct `dnf90.on_event`, `dnf90.on_ui_ready`, and `dnf90.on_ui_closed` callbacks. Dispatch order is generic callback, registered listeners in registration order, then the direct specific callback.
- Listener dispatch uses a stable snapshot: callbacks registered while an event is running begin with the next event, cancellation can suppress a later listener in the current snapshot, and `once` removes itself before invocation so callback re-entry cannot run it twice. Each listener has an independent protected call, so one error cannot stop remaining listeners or the direct callback. Hot reload replaces the listener table and therefore discards every old function while retaining a monotonic next ID, preventing a stale ID from cancelling a newly registered listener.
- Offline Lua validation proves registration order, one-shot removal, double-cancel results, listener-error isolation, deferred registration, invalid-name rejection, hot-reload cleanup, monotonic IDs `1..8`, zero default timers, and zero automatic notices. Source, Release, and configured-client `init.lua` copies are byte-identical at `13836` bytes with SHA256 `57015E7F3C1FD67E7F4E3696667726D168BD544109F9748ABCD6EC422EBA70D0`. The DLL binaries and compatibility manifest are unchanged; the preceding script is recoverable from `runtime/backups/client/20260803-111038-lua-listeners-predeploy`, and no DNF client was running during deployment. An initial BAT launch correctly refused while DNF90Server and MySQL were externally stopped; after that external workflow restored them as PIDs `13648/5192`, all 120 listeners passed preflight. Formal client PID `4252` then loaded the final script in the current EXE's Lua 5.1.5 state `0x093A2890` on worker `10096` and completed `ui_ready` sequence `1` from DNF UI thread `28596`; it remains running.

## 2026-08-03 - add safe Lua client-window lifecycle events

- Added a separate 32-entry lifecycle-event queue to `90CN.dll` and the undecorated `Dequeue90CNClientEvent` export. Each fixed ABI record carries its structure size, `ui_ready` or `ui_closed` type, process-local sequence, client process ID, and proved DNF UI thread ID. Main-window installation publishes `ui_ready` only after the existing window procedure and notice bridge are ready; `WM_DESTROY` publishes `ui_closed`, with `WM_NCDESTROY` as an idempotent fallback and bridge publication cleared at final destruction. A cancellable `WM_CLOSE` request is deliberately not treated as a closed window.

## 2026-08-01 - atomic trade gold/item projection and authoritative town party-menu state

- Corrected the current 28-byte class1/op19 item-trade interpretation from live 23.4.15.0 traffic. List0/slot0 with item instance zero and destination list4/slot0 is the real character-gold pseudo row, so it is now projected to the peer as the current 0x77-byte class0/op15 slot0/item0/count row instead of being rejected as a missing inventory item. Gold from both participants and both inventories commit through one two-character `CharacterTrade` transaction; insufficient balance and overflow fail closed, cancellation restores the virtual source mapping, completion refreshes each durable wallet, and neither side can commit independently.
- Corrected normal item offers in the same flow. The op19 source value is the current item-row instance/amount field rather than the PVF template ID; validation now compares it with the repository-backed 0x77 row value. This removes the false rejection that left the peer trade list blank while retaining binding, expiration, account-shared-slot, stack-count, and atomic destination-capacity checks.
- Made town party menu state actor-authoritative. Initial remote actor creation now sends that actor's real runtime op9 party-slot state rather than an unconditional empty display, and every create/join/leave/kick snapshot republishes the changed actor state to all same-area peers. Explicit op12 one-person parties stay active and therefore show “join party”; a multi-member party reduced to one member is fully disbanded for both affected actors, clearing the stale pointer that made a partyless target show “join party” instead of “invite to party”. No raid command or raid state is emitted by the ordinary-party path.

## 2026-07-27 - add the local account launcher and protected remembered credentials

- Added a standalone native Windows login GUI and the top-level `LOGIN.bat` entry. It supports account registration, username/password authentication, one-click server/client startup, and an optional “remember account and password” checkbox without adding a PowerShell deployment dependency.
- Added the loopback MySQL `dnf_login_accounts` identity table. Login names map to the existing DNF `account_id` boundary, passwords are stored only as bcrypt hashes, and the controller accepts passwords only from standard input. The first registered login claims the instance's current active account so upgraded `dnf:1` character data remains reachable; later registrations receive independent random account IDs.
- Kept the required local profile boundary: one DNF90Server process has one active account. A successful launcher login stops the existing local service when needed, authenticates the selected account, updates the active account ID through the controller, then starts the service and compatible client. This adds sequential multi-account switching without pretending the current protocol has simultaneous per-session account mapping.
- Remembered plaintext login credentials are stored only in the current Windows user's Credential Manager under a project-specific generic-credential target. They are not written to `instance.json`, MySQL, logs, command arguments, source, or release archives; a successful login with the checkbox cleared removes the saved credential.
- New release instances generate a random initial account ID instead of hard-coding `dnf:1`. Existing instances are not rewritten until an authenticated account switch, and the first registration preserves their current account data. The release builder, runtime build, documentation, and source-package whitelist now include `DNF90Launcher.exe` and `LOGIN.bat`.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, every `internal/modules/dnf/...` test, the complete `dnfbridge`, control, launcher, and release suites, Windows Credential Manager write/read/delete coverage, and the required `go vet` set. ## 2026-07-27 - serialize game-session state through a per-connection event loop

## 2026-07-27 - serialize the complete per-session game wire boundary

- Fixed a framework-level race between gameplay/time-queue sends and the game connection reader. The per-session wire lock now owns transport/upper sequence allocation, packet construction, DPROTO state transitions, and the corresponding socket write as one atomic operation; a later sender can no longer allocate an earlier sequence and then reach the socket after another packet.
- Routed ordinary latest-game transport, fixed-15 compatibility transport, character-roster upper, ordinary upper, native DPROTO decode/control output, and protected upper output through that same boundary. Fixed-header upper packets that deliberately do not consume a sequence retain their current wire layout and are still serialized at write time.

- Added concurrent regressions with 128 simultaneous latest-transport sends and 128 simultaneous upper sends. They parse the resulting byte stream and require exact wire-order sequences `0..127`; a separate 256-way regression verifies the packet-log counter. The previous revive-coin path no longer rebuilds all selected item containers through multiple op13 packets.

MySQL proved their `item_kind=stackable`, PVF paths, and counts were present; `amount_or_count` was being mistaken for an equipment quality seed before the stackable fallback ran. Quality aliases are now equipment-only unless an explicit `quality_seed` exists. Login/full-list and op14 incremental encoders preserve an explicit identity first and otherwise project the authoritative item id as the stable nonzero stackable wire identity without changing durable inventory contents.

Static current-EXE analysis then pinned the two main-inventory op44 callbacks at VA `0x015280C0` and `0x01B72FD0`, plus the native writer sequence `[u16 opcode=44][i16 slot][u8 list=0][u32 identity][u32 item][u32 reserved=0]`. `90CN.dll` now wraps only those exact callbacks, lets the native path run first, and emits the same 15-byte request through the EXE's own scalar writers only when no native op44 was produced and the selected row is an exact `premiumlist_new.etc [item]` id from the pinned runtime PVF. Ordinary stackables, boxes, equipment, warehouses, and non-premium items cannot enter the fallback.

That live failure is retained as the regression fixture's exact `item_kind + amount + amount_or_count + pvf_path` metadata combination rather than being treated as a successful release.
- Corrected the op208 single/display magic-box header from the running current EXE rather than the contradictory earlier notes. After the class1 dispatcher consumes success, `sub_1D05A60` always reads `clientType` and `doubleFlag`; type 0 reads its two u16 source slots at `0x01D05B8B/0x01D05B97` and enters the reward reader at `0x01D05DD3`, while the reported Seria type 4 reads them at `0x01D06815/0x01D06821` and enters the reward reader at `0x01D0698A`. op208 is therefore `[u8 success=1][u8 clientType][u8 doubleFlag][i16 boxSlot][i16 materialSlot][u16 rewardCount][0x77 rows]`. Both ordinary zero-flag and Seria type-4 paths now have exact header regressions, including the live single-open request `04 55 00 FF FF`.

Real `Script.pvf` verification pins the reported item `31` (霸王之契约 3天) to type 22 and 259200 seconds, plus the three-day 达人/成长/晶 contract rows. Select-character restoration now also emits every active runtime-PVF u8 contract type instead of silently dropping the additional 29/80/88/92/100 families; internal 魔王 slots remain folded into native type 58.

## 2026-07-26 - opt-in 90CN debugger compatibility module

- Added an explicit, default-off `90CN.dll -> 90CN-debug.dll` loader. The normal patch worker reads game-root `90CN.ini`; only `[debug] enabled=1` loads the absolute game-root DLL and calls the versioned `Install90CNDebugCompat` export. Loading is never performed inside `DllMain`, and missing/disabled/rejected modules do not block the normal client patch.
- Added a separate Win32 `90CN-debug.vcxproj`. The module validates each current x86 `ntdll` entry as an unmodified `MOV EAX, imm32` syscall stub before installing a five-byte trampoline. All four entries are prepared first, other process threads are suspended only for the bounded write transaction, and partial writes are rolled back before threads resume.
- The initial compatibility scope is limited to current-process `NtQueryInformationProcess` debug port/object/flags, `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadHideFromDebugger)`, `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemKernelDebuggerInformation)`, and debug-register values returned to in-process callers by `NtGetContextThread`. A low-frequency worker clears only `PEB.BeingDebugged` and the `0x70` debug-heap bits in `NtGlobalFlag`.
- Explicitly excluded debugger-process enumeration hiding, `NtSetContextThread` mutation, kernel drivers, TenSDK byte patches, the TerSafe allow-all stub, game protocol changes, packet changes, and database/PVF/server behavior. The debug module is not loaded when `90CN.ini` is absent or disabled.
- Added an isolated x86 smoke executable. Three consecutive runs passed pseudo-handle and real-handle process queries, `ThreadHideFromDebugger`, kernel-debugger state, simulated PEB attach flags, and a suspended thread with real `DR0/DR7` values whose in-process query was sanitized without intercepting `SetThreadContext`.
- Clean `Release|Win32` builds passed for `90CN.vcxproj`, `90CN-debug.vcxproj`, and `90CN-debug-smoke.vcxproj`. Build intermediate directories are isolated. The final production loader SHA256 is `226F257FD30263DD7C4EC4E5DE9A4526394CA9F52F5E4DA98B3A59B846A483AC`; the optional debug module SHA256 is `F2DC7FE294638DB4842D0F990AAB2A761E598D4B8F63024CF13E2E5E91AC7A68`. The smoke executable passed again after the final loader build.
- Installed both DLLs into the configured client root, enabled the module explicitly through game-root `90CN.ini`, updated the strict client manifest, and confirmed a live client loaded all four native query hooks plus the PEB scrubber before the normal 90CN compatibility hooks. A narrow log-format correction removed wide-string paths from the narrow trace logger, then the corrected loader was rebuilt and redeployed after closing the residual client/debugger processes. Server, database, PVF, generated runtime configuration, and IDB were not changed.
- Live validation completed against client PID `32336` with x32dbg attached after the debug module reported ready. A single fast-resume x86 hardware read/write breakpoint at the current process PEB `BeingDebugged` byte remained active for more than 30 seconds and reached `3076` hits; the client stayed responsive and protection error `4026` did not occur. The test hardware breakpoint was then removed, the client resumed normally, and x32dbg remained attached for continued debugging.

## 2026-07-24 - portable MySQL open-box release supersedes Docker workflow

- Removed the Docker controller and Compose definition. The Windows local profile now uses the bundled official MySQL Community Server 8.4.10 Windows x64 archive directly; Redis, Docker, PowerShell scripts, network downloads, and a system-wide VC++ runtime installation are not required.
- Added SHA256/size manifests for the MySQL archive and three Microsoft x64 application-local runtime DLLs. Startup verifies both manifests, extracts MySQL under this installation, copies the verified DLLs next to `mysqld.exe`, and validates the server binary with `mysqld.exe --version`.
- First-start initialization now creates an installation ID, plaintext local root password, one loopback `dnf_local` read/write database, runtime configs, and schema state. The controller owns database identity with server UUID, port, data directory, process creation time, executable hash, PID file, and listener PID checks.
- Hardened portable database lifecycle with a cross-process controller lock, atomic state writes, external data ownership state, interrupted-initialization preservation, launch-configuration digests, validated SQL identity before privileged operations, saved active credentials for safe stop after config drift, and graceful SQL `SHUTDOWN`. MySQL is launched with `--no-monitor`, so one managed PID equals the PID file and port listener.
- Hardened multi-binary rebuilds with random temporary paths and a hash-verified transaction journal. A complete candidate set is committed after interruption; a partial set is rolled back to the exact previous hashes. Unknown files and paths outside `runtime/bin` are never guessed or removed.
- Fixed the Windows graceful-stop exit race observed by the extracted-package acceptance test. When the managed process exits between the handle state check and executable query, the controller now waits briefly for the already-open synchronized handle; it reports stopped only after that handle is signaled. A reused or protected live PID still fails closed.
- A real clean first start extracted and initialized MySQL, prepared the schema, opened all 31 listeners, and reached `DNF90 is READY`. A stop preserved data, a second start reused it, and the final `--no-monitor` run proved one `mysqld.exe` with matching state/PID-file/listener identity. Repeated start was idempotent and final stop left both services and all managed listeners stopped.
- Synced the latest six dungeon discard/pickup source and regression-test files from the original project, including the final scene-mode rollback at 09:45 CST. Exact source hashes match the original tree.
- Final validation passed `gofmt`, the complete requested control/server/doctor/bridge/DNF module test set, `go vet`, and BAT `REBUILD`. The rebuilt runtime contains `DNF90Control.exe`, `DNF90Server.exe`, and `DNF90Doctor.exe`.

## 2026-07-25 - fixed the exact 600-second Error2 watchdog

- Reproduced the channel-session failure exactly at 600 seconds and captured `runtime/logs/client-35768-channel13-after-600s.png`. At popup time the game TCP connection was still `Established`, op1276/op1516/op1518 traffic continued, and the backend had not disconnected the client; this proved the visible Error2 was a client watchdog rather than a transport timeout.
- Current-EXE analysis identified the timer at `owner+0x70` with duration `600000` ms. A class0/op1 resident notice makes the client send class1/op1 and arms that timer. The first S2C class1/op1 initializes the endpoint-ready gate at `[owner+0xB0]+0x90`; a later duplicate S2C class1/op1 returns through the already-ready branch before clearing the timer. The gate also owns UDP/NAT and periodic op1276 state, so forcing it back to zero is not a valid fix.
- Removed all post-initialization class0/op1 notices from cold town entry, return-select entry, and channel reconnect. Each game TCP now receives exactly one connection-level S2C class1/op1. Genuine channel reconnect op4 immediately executes the complete target-owner town route; stale native op1/590 and op1/598 probes are logged and ignored, with no delay and no reply. Removed the obsolete reconnect endpoint-wait/display-pending state.
- Strengthened bridge regression coverage to forbid op1 anywhere in an initialized town/reconnect route, require target owner bytes and exactly one reconnect mode1/op800/op9/op120 sequence, preserve persisted location and NeedQuest state, keep op1276 ACK-only, and verify stale 590/598 probes cannot emit a response.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, focused bridge/DNF/server tests, `go vet`, and the production server build. The BAT-only controller rebuilt and restarted the local runtime; portable MySQL PID `45784` and DNF90Server PID `24312` reached READY without changing database data.
- Live client PID `42900` connected to channel 21 at `04:47:48`, entered town at `04:55:47`, and remained responsive for 705 seconds. TCP `192.168.1.113:57421 -> 192.168.1.113:10021` stayed `Established`; the entire session contained one initial S2C op1, zero C2S op1, and zero class0/op1. op1276/op1516/op1518 continued after the former deadline, and the server had no runtime disconnect, read/write error, panic, or fatal.
- Passing screenshot: `runtime/logs/client-42900-after-fix-620s.png` shows the live town scene after the former 600-second deadline with no Error2 popup.

## 2026-07-25 - permanent pet rows, pet-feed routing, and full equipment-quality seeds

- Corrected the op81 interpretation from the preceding deployment. Values `1..5` are not current-client grade enums; they are invalidly tiny quality seeds, which made every adjustment resolve near the bottom grade. Standard boxes now persist a cryptographically generated seed in `1..999999997`, while gold boxes persist the `999999998` top-quality sentinel.
- Current EXE `sub_1D16480` applies op81's nonzero u32 through the inventory object's stack mutation path, explaining the visible number added to the target after every reset. The success ACK now uses zero to discard that stale object and follows with a full class0/op13 main-container refresh. ## 2026-07-25 - same-session finish-quest replay suppression (client crash fix)

## 2026-07-25 - 86JP magic-box flow: seria batch ack, legacy popup, SeriaLuck doubling

- Adopted the 86JP reference flow (Desktop 86JP 最新整合版, `MagicBoxOpenAckBuilder` + `InventoryHandler.Package.cs` + `InventoryPackageStore.cs`), with packet structure pinned to current-EXE IDA evidence wherever the two disagree:
  - Seria source (2682272) expand (0x0468): native batch ack `[u8 variant=4][u8 doubleFlag][u16 openCount][i16 boxSlot][i16 materialSlot=-1][list1: u16 n + n×0x77 rows][u16 0][list2: doubles]`. variant=4 matches `sub_1D074C0` (skips the bogus material check that fired error 11031; the double list feeds the seria two-pane result branch).

Applies to both single (208) and expand opens; the single ack's doubleFlag byte now reflects it.

## 2026-07-26 - expand display unified on the proven 208 channel; dispatch evidence confirms handler delivery

- Patch dispatch trace (newly observed opcodes) proves both the 0x0468 expand ack and the 0x00A0 popup reach their registered client handlers (RVA 0x19074C0/0x192BFF0 -> VA 0x1D074C0/0x1D2BFF0) and run to completion, so the display failure was inside the window layer, not packet routing.
- 0x00A0/0x145 and the 0x36B seria window do not materialize renderable rows in this client build, so expand display is unified on the proven single-open channel: every expand now sends its native ack (seria batch variant=4 for 2682272, ordinary variant=1 ack otherwise) followed by the 208-shaped display ack with aggregated rewards. SeriaLuck doubling, material checks, and the container refresh are unchanged.
- Tests: teddy expects [1128 expand ack, 208 display]; seria expects [1128 batch ack, 208 display with doubleFlag=1 and the doubled round aggregated]; failure ack unchanged.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem` and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - single/display ack reward rows are 0x77 wire entries, not 31-byte summaries

- Root cause of the one-row result window: the current EXE reads every magic-box reward list with `sub_1D057B0`, which is hardwired to 0x77-byte item entries. The 86JP BuildSingle 31-byte summary rows parsed as one valid entry followed by garbage, so the window only ever rendered the first reward (teddy showed potions without seria; single opens showed only row one).
- `buildMagicBoxSingleAck` now emits 0x77 wire entries (u16 slot, u32 itemID, u32 count, zero tail) with real granted slots; the 208 single-open and the expand display acks share this shape. The seria batch ack already used 0x77 rows and its double pane renders correctly (red 双倍 window confirmed live).
- Tests updated to 0x77-row expectations for single, teddy-expand, and seria-batch flows.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem` and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - seria two-pane window: left = base draws, right = doubled round

- The seria double window rendered only the red (double) pane because the left list carried the aggregated rewards *including* the doubled round, duplicating the right list; the client merge emptied the left pane. Per 86JP `GetPrimaryRewards` semantics, the batch ack's first list is now `DisplayRewards` (base draws only) and the second list stays `DoubleRewards` (the triggered round), so both panes render.
- Owner tracks base-draw aggregation separately (`result.DisplayRewards`) without changing grant totals (doubled rounds are still granted twice into inventory).
- Tests updated: 10 opens from luck 0 -> left list 20, double list 2, doubleFlag=1.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem` and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - non-seria expand drops the native 1128 ack (kills the bogus announcement)

- The 1128 handler (`sub_1D074C0`) unconditionally ends with a follow-up announcement (strings 70156/70157, rendered as a shop-cross-class warning by the 2018 `dstr.dat`). Non-seria expand opens don't need that packet at all: the result window is fully carried by the 208 display ack, and the client showed no wait-state hang when 1128 was previously unimplemented. Teddy and other non-seria expands now receive only the 208 display ack, so the result window appears without the bogus dialog. Seria keeps the native batch ack (required for its two-pane window) plus the 208 display ack; its follow-up text remains a known string-table issue to fix separately.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem` and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - 208 single ack has no doubleFlag byte (current-EXE read layout)

- The current EXE single-open handler (`sub_1D05A60`) reads two u8s (result, clientType) then u16 slots — there is no doubleFlag byte. Writing one shifted every later field, so any open with a triggered seria double (doubleFlag=1) made the client look up a garbage box slot and the normal result window never appeared. Removed the byte; the ack is now `[u8 1][u8 clientType][u16 boxSlot][u16 materialSlot][u16 count][count × 0x77 rows]`. DoubleFlag=0 cases accidentally worked before because the byte aliased the slot's high byte.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem` and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - equipment magic-box rewards: top-quality seed and PVF durability (86JP ItemQuality)

- The result window rendered equipment reward rows as "加载中" because the rows carried a stack count where the client expects equipment instance data. Per 86JP `ItemQuality.TopQualitySeed` (999999998) and `GenerateInstanceValue`, equipment magic-box rewards now grant with `quality_seed=999999998` plus PVF `[durability]` in the stack extras (count forced to 1), and display rows write the quality seed at 0x06 and durability at 0x0B instead of a count. The resolver now extracts `[durability]` from the reward item's PVF (`MagicBoxRewardItem.Durability`).
- Seria pool group-3 equipment draws (25xxxxxx) therefore land in the bag with proper equipment metadata and render in the result window.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `packageitem`, `alignedcmd`, and full `internal/services/dnfbridge` suites, `go vet`; rebuilt via `REBUILD.bat` and restarted through the BAT controller.

## 2026-07-26 - gameplay file modules and unified gameplay time queue

- Split gameplay entry routing out of the legacy and upper transport switches into 17 independently registered gameplay files: NPC shop, Cera shop/package, inventory, skill, disjoint/emblem, booster, rental, equipment upgrades/sockets, aura skin, quest, dungeon, town, adventure info, title, achievement, emotion, and collectbox. The central dispatchers now retain only connection, login, roster, character lifecycle, compatibility probes, and fallback transport responsibilities.
- Each gameplay module owns its legacy/upper opcode routes and any legacy transport-body normalization. The registry rejects duplicate module names, duplicate opcode ownership, and duplicate normalizer ownership at startup; regression coverage pins representative owners and normalization boundaries.
- Replaced the dungeon-only timer adapter with one process-wide gameplay time queue. The ten-second death return, 750 ms tutorial-final grace, and pet satiety 60-second dungeon decay/360-second town recovery ticks now schedule, replace, and cancel through that queue. Dungeon start, clear, settlement, hunt completion, and pet elapsed calculations use the same injectable queue clock. Durable item, rental, premium, mail, and skill-cooldown deadlines remain authoritative MySQL timestamps and are evaluated by their owners; network deadlines, database timeouts, process supervision, and publisher retries remain infrastructure timers outside the gameplay queue.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, every DNF module test, the complete bridge/control suites, the requested `go vet` set, the production build, and BAT offline/online preflight. ## 2026-08-04 - fix joust match profile keys that crashed town login

- A current-client town-login crash at `09:45:14` mapped `0xC0000005` through the EXE's deliberate null-write abort stub to the type-73 match updater `sub_F2EE20`. The failing round `51420` began with rider 4 defeating rider 8 while the server sent generated profile keys `5/3`; the current EXE has only four PVF-backed profile vectors and the absent key 5 produced an empty timeline followed by the native invalid-parameter abort.
- The seven monotonic values in each PVF battle group are client timeline percentages, not seven independently selectable action IDs. Tournament match records now send the opponent attack-type group keys proved by the EXE/PVF pairing: attack types `0`, `1`, `27`, and `28` map to profile keys `0..3`. Round 51420's first match is consequently the valid `4/1` versus `8/1` pair, and deterministic winners, bracket progression, odds, betting, history, and settlement remain unchanged.
- Regression coverage checks every `uint16` round against each participant's opponent attack type and pins the real-PVF crash round. Validation passed `gofmt`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, all DNF module tests, focused Joust/bridge tests, the control suite, the requested `go vet` set, the real runtime PVF smoke test, the production build, and BAT-only offline/online preflight. The full bridge suite still reports four pre-existing town teleport/crystal packet-order failures outside this change. The relational database was preserved; the prior server is backed up under `runtime/backups/server/20260804-joust-action-profile-login-crash-predeploy`, portable MySQL PID `27664` and DNF90Server PID `24896` are READY, all 120 listeners accept connections, fresh stderr is empty, and the deployed server SHA256 is `03B1816DB9D361527B06B60F34F94535603B32B2F703106A66E5AEF52C2B2295`.

## 2026-08-05 - current EXE weapon-effect rune application

- Current NoPack live traffic for `CmdPacketAddEquipmentEffect` (951) is now handled as its proved 21-byte request: main-inventory target list at byte 12, target slot at byte 13, and source slot at byte 17. The success response preserves the exact native shape: one success byte followed by the original request body.
- Added the `equipmenteffect` domain owner. It transactionally validates a PVF `[equipment effect]` stackable and its `[int data]` effect ID, verifies that the target is an unsealed un-equipped PVF weapon above normal/high grade, consumes one rune, and records the effect durably. A historical client row-slot mismatch is recovered only when there is exactly one real effect rune in the main inventory; ambiguous state fails closed, so an unrelated material is never consumed.
- The original projection location stated here (`raw 0x38`) was later disproved by the current EXE reader: it is the expiration timestamp, not a rune field. That implementation is superseded by the corrected `raw 0x45` mapping documented below. The request transaction and persistence behavior remain valid.

## 2026-08-05 - project weapon-effect rune into equipped-item tooltips

- The first effect-rune deployment correctly stored `equipment_effect_id=1` on the target weapon and consumed the peach rune, but only the main-inventory 0x77 projection wrote the tail rune field. After the player wore the weapon, list-3/mode1 used `currentItemListEntryFromEquipment` and therefore the native tooltip had no effect line.
- The equipped projection work recorded here used the same subsequently disproved `raw 0x38` location and is superseded by the corrected mapping below.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, focused bridge and equipment-effect owner tests, `go vet ./internal/services/dnfbridge`, and the production build. BAT-only deployment preserved the relational database; the prior image is under `runtime/backups/server/20260805-equipment-effect-equipped-tooltip-predeploy`, portable MySQL PID `30996` and DNF90Server PID `33672` are READY, all 120 listeners accept connections, fresh stderr is empty, and deployed server SHA256 is `9E006611BEB41B8A2EA466866B7A9306CADD979BE1D485EF4FBE2153C1AEAB15`.

## 2026-08-05 - correct current-client weapon-effect rune hydration

- The missing 桃花 tooltip line was traced to two independent current-client mistakes: the current `90CN.dll` scene-route allowlist excluded `CmdPacketAddEquipmentEffect` (951), so its native success handler never ran; and the prior server projection wrote raw `0x38`, which the current 0x77 reader uses for expiration.
- The durable rune ID now maps to raw `0x45..0x46`: the proved current emblem vector occupies raw `0x3C..0x44`, and mode1 forwards the following word into its ordinary-equipment state. Both bag and worn-equipment 0x77 projection preserve raw `0x38` expiration and set/clear only this rune word. After mode1 creates equipment objects, a single list-3/op14 refresh includes only equipped entries that actually have `equipment_effect_id`.
- The Release/Win32 DLL admits opcode 951 to the native route. Validation passed `gofmt`, package listing, every DNF-module test, the complete bridge/control suites, the requested `go vet` set, the focused rune regression, a Release/Win32 DLL build, and a server candidate build (`runtime/tmp/DNF90Server-equipment-effect-rune-0x45-candidate.exe`, SHA256 `7491F8F2371A3CA361D60164C7DF5E4A55ECCD5D7DC3CA629A6280F28EB02211`). After the game was closed, the prior runtime DLL and EXE were preserved under `runtime/backups/20260805-equipment-effect-rune-0x45-predeploy`; the deployed DLL and server both match their verified candidates (DLL SHA256 `33BA46E898D592CACAD5B3774A682660BF1194DADB80332456C4773A781652CE`, EXE SHA256 `7491F8F2371A3CA361D60164C7DF5E4A55ECCD5D7DC3CA629A6280F28EB02211`). BAT-only startup reports MySQL PID `30996` and DNF90Server PID `33344` READY with all 120 listeners accepting connections. No database state was modified during deployment.

## 2026-08-05 - Cera package overflow delivery

- The 2018 Beast Guardian Cera package (`490701964`) is now opened through the mailbox asset transaction. If a normal PVF reward category is full, its complete reward row becomes a durable, non-expiring system-mail attachment while the source package, all direct rewards, and the mail commit atomically.
- Direct placement is first probed on a cloned inventory. This prevents the former all-or-nothing rejection and also prevents a partial stack merge from being duplicated when the remainder must be mailed. Avatar, creature, and pet-consumable placement keeps its existing authoritative category handling.
- The current client receives its normal op518 success and inventory refresh, then a mailbox alarm; delayed alarm delivery is harmless because the saved mailbox snapshot remains authoritative. Tests cover atomic main-bag overflow with preserved item expiry/raw state and the live PVF's 28 rows, 996 total units, eight avatar choices, coupon, material, and pet-feed rows.
- Validation passed `gofmt`, `git diff --check`, `go list -buildvcs=false ./...`, all DNF module tests, the full bridge and control suites, the requested `go vet` set, and a real-PVF smoke test. The deployed server SHA256 is `7491F8F2371A3CA361D60164C7DF5E4A55ECCD5D7DC3CA629A6280F28EB02211`; the previous image is backed up under `runtime/backups/server/20260805-cera-package-overflow-predeploy`. BAT preflight passed, the readiness endpoint reports HTTP 200, portable MySQL remains PID `30996`, and DNF90Server is READY as PID `33344`.

## 2026-08-05 - party leave authority and return-town co-presence recovery

- A party membership mutation that leaves one manager-side member no longer immediately reprojects that singleton through the client party packets. The central manager keeps the short-lived invite anchor, but the remaining client receives the native empty party projection, preventing the stale non-leader dungeon-entry gate after leave, kick, disconnect, or cross-party join.
- Leave packets are consumed even when the old client cache cannot be imported. The bridge sends the successful leave acknowledgement and clears that stale projection instead of falling through to the legacy handler, so an already-desynchronized client returns to a solo state deterministically.
- After a party returns from the dungeon-selection page, each client waits for every same-area member to consume its own op132 scene-finalizer. It then reconstructs remote actors and rebinds the realtime roster in native order, restoring town visibility and the party/minimap roster without injecting actors into a peer that is still on the selector page.
- Focused bridge regression coverage now pins leader leave, kick, and cross-party-join residual singleton clearing, alongside the existing selector synchronization and runtime-party authority tests. This entry documents source changes only; no running server has been replaced while players remain connected.
